     7→last_validated: 2026-02-13
     8→last_validated_by: Claude
     9→---
    10→
    11→# Dual — Engineering Requirements (Dual)
    12→
    13→Terminal workspace orchestrator for parallel multi-repo development with AI coding agents.
    14→
    15→---
    16→
    17→## CRITICAL: The Core Invariant
    18→
    19→**Claude Code must never know it is running inside a container.**
    20→
    21→Every architectural decision flows from this constraint. Claude runs `pnpm dev`. Claude curls `localhost:3000`. Claude runs `npx playwright test`. All of these must work exactly as they would on a bare metal machine.
    22→
    23→- DO NOT expose Docker to Claude Code — Claude never sees containers, images, or port mappings
    24→- DO NOT require Claude to check environment variables, remember mapped ports, or do anything non-standard
    25→- DO NOT leak container abstractions through error messages, paths, or process names
    26→- ONLY present an environment where `localhost:{port}` is the real service, because it is — the command executes inside the container's network namespace
    27→- If Claude has to remember anything about the infrastructure, the system has failed
    28→
    29→---
    30→
    31→## CRITICAL: The Equation
    32→
    33→**One monorepo worktree = one container = one machine.**
    34→
    35→- DO NOT split monorepo services into separate containers
    36→- DO NOT use docker-compose per project
    37→- DO NOT create per-service isolation within a worktree
    38→- ONLY create one container per worktree clone
    39→- The developer runs `pnpm dev` and the monorepo's own tooling (turbo, nx) starts all services inside that single container on their default ports
    40→- All services share the same `localhost` inside the container — they talk to each other normally
    41→
    42→---
    43→
    44→## CRITICAL: Parallel Execution
    45→
    46→All workspaces run simultaneously. There is no concept of a single "active" workspace.
    47→
    48→- DO NOT design around one workspace being active at a time
    49→- DO NOT require stopping one workspace to start another
    50→- DO NOT share state, ports, or resources between workspaces
    51→- 5 repos × 3 worktrees each = 15 simultaneous environments, all with Claude Code sessions active, all running dev servers on default ports, with zero conflicts
    52→- Each container has its own network namespace — 15 containers can all bind `:3000` simultaneously
    53→
    54→---
    55→
    56→## Architecture Overview
    57→
    58→Dual is the orchestration layer between the terminal emulator and the runtime. It does not replace tmux. It does not replace Docker. It replaces how the developer interacts with both.
    59→
    60→### System Layers (outermost → innermost)
    61→
    62→| Layer | What It Is | Who Owns It |
    63→|---|---|---|
    64→| Terminal Emulator | Ghostty, iTerm, Kitty, Alacritty — provides the rendering surface | User's choice |
    65→| **Dual** | Workspace switching, clone lifecycle, container lifecycle, command routing, reverse proxy | **This project** |
    66→| Isolation Layer | Docker — one headless container per worktree, all services inside | Docker (orchestrated by Dual) |
    67→| Reverse Proxy | Dual subprocess — maps `*.localhost:{port}` to containers | **This project** |
    68→| Runtime Backend | tmux/zellij on the host — keeps dev tool panes alive across workspace switches | tmux (orchestrated by Dual) |
    69→| Workspace Processes | Split: dev tools on host (nvim, claude, git) · all services in container (`pnpm dev`) | Split ownership |
    70→
    71→### Two Audiences, Two Paths
    72→
    73→There are exactly two consumers of a workspace's running services. They access them differently.
    74→
    75→**Claude Code (and all CLI tools) → Container Network**
    76→
    77→All runtime commands execute inside the container via `dual run` → `docker exec`. Inside the container, `localhost:3000` is the real service. 15 Claude sessions can all run `pnpm dev` on `:3000` simultaneously because each container has its own network namespace.
    78→
    79→**Developer (browser) → Multi-Port Reverse Proxy**
    80→
    81→Dual runs a lightweight reverse proxy on the host. The proxy listens on all ports that any container is using. Subdomain selects the container: `lightfast-feat-auth.localhost:3001` routes to the `dual-lightfast-feat-auth` container's `:3001`. `*.localhost` resolves to `127.0.0.1` natively in all modern browsers — no `/etc/hosts`, no DNS config, no browser extensions.
    82→
    83→---
    84→
    85→## Command Routing
    86→
    87→Dual maintains a routing table that determines where each command executes.
    88→
    89→### The Rule: File Operations → Host. Runtime Operations → Container.
    90→
    91→| Command | Executes On | Why |
    92→|---|---|---|
    93→| `edit src/app.tsx` | Host | Bind mount — container sees the change via shared filesystem |
    94→| `git commit -m "fix"` | Host | Uses developer's credentials and SSH keys |
    95→| `cat package.json` | Host | Read-only file operation |
    96→| `pnpm dev` | Container | Binds ports — must be in isolated network namespace |
    97→| `pnpm install` | Container | Writes to `node_modules` inside container |
    98→| `curl localhost:3000` | Container | Needs access to the container's network |
    99→| `npx playwright test` | Container | Needs both browser runtime and network access |
   100→
   101→### Defaults and Configuration
   102→
   103→- Anything `npm`/`pnpm`/`node`/`python`/`curl` → container
   104→- Anything `git`/`cat`/`ls`/`vim`/`nvim` → host
   105→- Configurable per project via workspace config
   106→- The mechanism: `dual run <command>` wraps `docker exec <container-name> <command>`
   107→- Claude Code's shell is configured so that commands are intercepted and routed — Claude never sees `dual run` or `docker exec`
   108→
   109→---
   110→
   111→## Git Strategy
   112→
   113→### CRITICAL: Full Clones, Not Worktrees
   114→
   115→Every workspace is a full `git clone`. Each has its own `.git/` directory, its own `node_modules`, its own build artifacts, its own env files.
   116→
   117→- DO NOT use `git worktree` — shared `.git` object store creates lock contention when 15 sessions are active
   118→- DO NOT share refs across workspaces — one bad rebase can corrupt all worktrees
   119→- DO NOT share stash, index, or configs across workspaces
   120→
   121→**Why not worktrees:**
   122→- Shared `.git` object store creates lock contention with 15 active sessions
   123→- Shared refs mean one bad rebase can corrupt all worktrees
   124→- Cannot have two worktrees on the same branch
   125→- Stash, index, and some configs are shared — causes unexpected side effects
   126→- Claude modifying `.git` state in one worktree can break another
   127→
   128→**Clone strategy:**
   129→- Remote origin → `git clone <url>` (standard network clone)
   130→- Local path → `git clone --local <path>` (uses hardlinks — fast, low disk overhead)
   131→- Optional `--depth N` for large repos where full history is not needed
   132→
   133→### Filesystem Layout
   134→
   135→```
   136→~/dual-workspaces/
   137→├─ lightfast-platform/          ← project (repo)
   138→│  ├─ main/                     ← full clone, own .git, own node_modules
   139→│  ├─ feat__auth/               ← full clone, completely independent
   140→│  └─ fix__memory-leak/         ← full clone
   141→└─ agent-os/                    ← project (repo)
   142→   ├─ main/
   143→   └─ v2-rewrite/
   144→```
   145→
   146→- Branch names use double-underscore as separator (`feat/auth` → `feat__auth`) because filesystem paths cannot contain `/`
   147→- Each clone directory is completely independent — no shared state of any kind
   148→
   149→---
   150→
   151→## Container Lifecycle
   152→
   153→### Zero-Config Image Generation
   154→
   155→Dual inspects the monorepo and auto-builds a minimal container image.
   156→
   157→- DO NOT require Dockerfiles from the developer
   158→- DO NOT include dev tools (nvim, claude, git) in the container — those stay on the host
   159→- ONLY install what's needed to run `pnpm dev`
   160→
   161→| Detects | Provisions in Container |
   162→|---|---|
   163→| `.nvmrc` / `.node-version` | Node.js (exact version) |
   164→| `pnpm-lock.yaml` | pnpm + turbo/nx |
   165→| `pyproject.toml` | Python + uv/pip |
   166→| `docker-compose.yml` | Parses service definitions → postgres, redis, etc. |
   167→| `playwright.config.*` | Chromium + browser dependencies |
   168→| `.env` / `.env.local` | Environment variables |
   169→| `turbo.json` / `nx.json` | Monorepo task runner |
   170→
   171→### Image Caching
   172→
   173→- Image is cached per monorepo — all worktrees of the same repo share the same base image
   174→- Rebuilt only when dependencies change (lockfile hash, node version, etc.)
   175→
   176→### Container Naming
   177→
   178→Pattern: `dual-{repo}-{branch}`
   179→
   180→Examples:
   181→- `dual-lightfast-main`
   182→- `dual-lightfast-feat-auth`
   183→- `dual-agent-os-feat-memory`
   184→
   185→### Bind Mount
   186→
   187→The worktree directory on the host is bind-mounted into the container:
   188→- File edits on the host (via nvim or Claude) are immediately visible inside the container
   189→- The dev server's hot reload picks up changes instantly
   190→- `node_modules` and build artifacts live inside the container's filesystem
   191→- The source tree is shared; the runtime state is isolated
   192→
   193→---
   194→
   195→## Reverse Proxy
   196→
   197→### CRITICAL: Multi-Port Subdomain Routing
   198→
   199→The proxy does not run on a single port. It binds to every port that any container is using.
   200→
   201→- DO NOT use a single gateway port with path-based routing
   202→- DO NOT require the developer to remember port mappings
   203→- DO NOT require any host-side configuration (no `/etc/hosts`, no DNS, no browser extensions)
   204→- ONLY use subdomain + port to select the target: `{repo}-{branch}.localhost:{port}` → container's `:{port}`
   205→
   206→### URL Pattern
   207→
   208→```
   209→{repo}-{branch}.localhost:{port}
   210→```
   211→
   212→### How It Works
   213→
   214→1. Proxy is a Dual subprocess — starts automatically when Dual starts, zero config
   215→2. When a container starts and binds ports (`:3000`, `:3001`, `:4001`), Dual registers those ports with the proxy
   216→3. Proxy starts listening on any newly registered ports (if not already listening)
   217→4. Request arrives at `lightfast-feat-auth.localhost:3001`:
   218→   - Proxy reads the `Host` header → `lightfast-feat-auth.localhost`
   219→   - Proxy reads the port → `:3001`
   220→   - Proxy looks up the container → `dual-lightfast-feat-auth`
   221→   - Proxy forwards the request to that container's `:3001`
   222→5. When containers stop, their port registrations are removed. If no other container uses that port, the proxy stops listening on it.
   223→
   224→### Requirements
   225→
   226→- MUST support HTTP and WebSocket (for hot reload / HMR)
   227→- MUST support SSE (Server-Sent Events) and streaming responses
   228→- MUST update routing table dynamically as containers start and stop
   229→- MUST handle concurrent connections to multiple containers simultaneously
   230→- Implementation: Caddy, Traefik, or a custom ~150 line Go/Node HTTP proxy
   231→
   232→### Example: Full URL Table
   233→
   234→For 2 monorepos × 3 worktrees each (6 containers, 18+ services):
   235→
   236→```
   237→lightfast-main.localhost:3000           → www (marketing site)
   238→lightfast-main.localhost:3001           → app (dashboard)
   239→lightfast-main.localhost:4001           → api (backend)
   240→lightfast-main.localhost:3002           → docs
   241→
   242→lightfast-feat-auth.localhost:3000      → www
   243→lightfast-feat-auth.localhost:3001      → app
   244→lightfast-feat-auth.localhost:4001      → api
   245→lightfast-feat-auth.localhost:3002      → docs
   246→
   247→lightfast-fix-billing.localhost:3000    → www
   248→lightfast-fix-billing.localhost:3001    → app
   249→lightfast-fix-billing.localhost:4001    → api
   250→lightfast-fix-billing.localhost:3002    → docs
   251→
   252→agent-os-main.localhost:8080            → runtime (engine)
   253→agent-os-main.localhost:3000            → studio (visual builder)
   254→agent-os-main.localhost:9000            → gateway (API)
   255→
   256→agent-os-feat-memory.localhost:8080     → runtime
   257→agent-os-feat-memory.localhost:3000     → studio
   258→agent-os-feat-memory.localhost:9000     → gateway
   259→
   260→agent-os-perf-hotpath.localhost:8080    → runtime
   261→agent-os-perf-hotpath.localhost:3000    → studio
   262→agent-os-perf-hotpath.localhost:9000    → gateway
   263→```
   264→
   265→All on default ports inside their containers. All accessible simultaneously from the developer's browser.
   266→
   267→---
   268→
   269→## Runtime Backend Contract
   270→
   271→The runtime backend is an abstraction over the terminal multiplexer. Dual ships with a tmux backend as the default, but the interface is designed to be swappable.
   272→
   273→### Interface
   274→
   275→| Method | Signature | Description |
   276→|---|---|---|
   277→| `create_session` | `(workspace_id, processes[])` | Create a new runtime session with the given processes. Returns a session handle. |
   278→| `attach` | `(session_handle)` | Connect the current terminal to this session. User sees the processes. |
   279→| `detach` | `(session_handle)` | Disconnect terminal from session. Processes keep running in background. |
   280→| `destroy` | `(session_handle)` | Kill all processes and tear down the session. |
   281→| `is_alive` | `(session_handle) → bool` | Check if the session still has running processes. |
   282→| `list_sessions` | `() → session_handle[]` | Return all managed sessions. |
   283→
   284→### Implementations
   285→
   286→| Backend | Status | Description |
   287→|---|---|---|
   288→| `TmuxBackend` | Default | Each session = tmux session. Processes = tmux panes. Attach/detach is native tmux. |
   289→| `ZellijBackend` | Future | Same model, different multiplexer. |
   290→| `BasicBackend` | Fallback | Background processes only. No multiplexing or panes. |
   291→
   292→### Progressive Enhancement
   293→
   294→If a user doesn't have tmux, Dual still works. They get clone management, workspace switching, container lifecycle, and the reverse proxy — just without pane layouts. The `BasicBackend` is the floor. Multiplexer backends are progressive enhancements.
   295→
   296→---
   297→
   298→## User Experience
   299→
   300→### First Launch
   301→
   302→1. Developer opens terminal emulator
   303→2. Runs `dual` — it becomes their session
   304→3. Dual shows fuzzy picker across all projects/workspaces
   305→4. Developer selects a workspace
   306→5. Dual: creates clone if needed → starts container → creates tmux session → attaches
   307→6. Developer lands in their workspace with nvim, claude, and shell running
   308→
   309→### Workspace Switching (The Meta-Key Moment)
   310→
   311→1. Developer presses the meta key
   312→2. Current workspace is "frozen" (detached from runtime — processes keep running)
   313→3. Dual overlay appears with fuzzy picker
   314→4. Shows: project name, branch, session status (● running / ○ stopped)
   315→5. Developer types to filter, presses enter to select
   316→6. Dual attaches to the new workspace's runtime session
   317→7. If no session exists, Dual creates one (clone + container + launch processes)
   318→8. Loops back — developer can switch again at any time
   319→
   320→The developer never leaves Dual's control. They never run `tmux attach`. They never run `docker exec`. They never manage sessions manually.
   321→
   322→### CLI Commands
   323→
   324→```
   325→dual                          → launch (shows fuzzy picker)
   326→dual open                     → opens all services for current workspace in browser
   327→dual open lightfast-main      → opens all lightfast-main services in browser
   328→dual urls                     → lists all running workspace URLs
   329→dual urls lightfast-feat-auth → URLs for that specific workspace
   330→```
   331→
   332→#### `dual urls` Output
   333→
   334→```
   335→lightfast-feat-auth  ● running
   336→  www      lightfast-feat-auth.localhost:3000
   337→  app      lightfast-feat-auth.localhost:3001
   338→  api      lightfast-feat-auth.localhost:4001
   339→  docs     lightfast-feat-auth.localhost:3002
   340→
   341→agent-os-feat-memory  ● running
   342→  studio   agent-os-feat-memory.localhost:3000
   343→  runtime  agent-os-feat-memory.localhost:8080
   344→  gateway  agent-os-feat-memory.localhost:9000
   345→```
   346→
   347→---
   348→
   349→## Workspace States
   350→
   351→| State | Description |
   352→|---|---|
   353→| **ATTACHED** | Runtime session is connected to your terminal. You see and interact with processes. |
   354→| **BACKGROUND** | Processes still running (Claude is thinking, dev server is serving) but not displayed. |
   355→| **STOPPED** | No runtime session. Clone exists on disk. Session created on first access. |
   356→| **LAZY** | Config-only. No clone on disk. Clone + session created on first access. |
   357→
   358→---
   359→
   360→## What Dual Does NOT Do
   361→
   362→- Does NOT replace tmux/zellij — it orchestrates them
   363→- Does NOT replace Docker — it orchestrates it
   364→- Does NOT require Dockerfiles — it auto-generates container images
   365→- Does NOT expose Docker to Claude Code — Claude never knows containers exist
   366→- Does NOT require port remapping awareness from any tool
   367→- Does NOT require `/etc/hosts` changes or DNS configuration
   368→- Does NOT run dev tools (nvim, claude, git) inside containers — those stay on the host
   369→- Does NOT manage a single "active" workspace — all workspaces run simultaneously
   370→
   371→---
   372→
   373→## Implementation Phases
   374→
   375→### Phase 1: Core Loop
   376→- Workspace config format and discovery
   377→- Full clone management (`git clone --local`)
   378→- tmux backend (create, attach, detach, destroy)
   379→- Meta-key workspace switcher with fuzzy picker
   380→- Filesystem layout (`~/dual-workspaces/{repo}/{branch}/`)
   381→
   382→### Phase 2: Isolation
   383→- Container image auto-generation from project files
   384→- Container lifecycle management (create, start, stop, destroy)
   385→- Bind mount configuration
   386→- Command routing (`dual run` → `docker exec`)
   387→- Shell integration for transparent command interception
   388→
   389→### Phase 3: Network Access
   390→- Multi-port reverse proxy
   391→- Dynamic port registration as containers start/stop
   392→- Subdomain routing (`*.localhost`)
   393→- WebSocket and SSE support
   394→- `dual open` and `dual urls` commands
   395→
   396→### Phase 4: Polish
   397→- TUI with workspace sidebar showing live status
   398→- `dual urls` in TUI sidebar
   399→- Container image caching and rebuild detection
   400→- Zellij backend
   401→- `BasicBackend` for users without a multiplexer
   402→
```

{"parentUuid":"b84db5b1-8746-4cc8-9898-bf3957e7459f","isSidechain":true,"userType":"external","cwd":"/Users/jeevanpillay/Code/@jeevanpillaystudios/dual","sessionId":"b54b5261-ab92-4c9e-9482-0621f9c1b755","version":"2.1.42","gitBranch":"main","agentId":"a62f6a8","slug":"fizzy-whistling-willow","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01KD8uQ8oskXnUGXDGygxz65","type":"tool_result","content":"# Develop Applications - Ratatui\n\nThis documentation page provides an overview of recipes for building terminal applications using Ratatui, a Rust library for creating user interfaces in the terminal.\n\n## Key Topics Covered\n\nThe page outlines nine main recipe categories for application development:\n\n1. **CLI Arguments** - Handling command-line input\n2. **Configuration Directories** - Managing app configuration files\n3. **Logging with Tracing** - Implementing structured logging\n4. **Terminal and Event Handler** - Organizing core application code\n5. **Panic Hooks** - Handling application crashes gracefully\n6. **Better Panic** - Enhanced error handling\n7. **Migrating from TUI-rs** - Upgrading from the predecessor library\n8. **Spawning External Editors** - Integrating tools like Vim\n9. **Release Preparation** - Publishing your application\n\n## Purpose\n\nAccording to the navigation structure, this section focuses on \"recipes for building ratatui applications, especially around event loops and terminal state management.\" These guides help developers establish best practices for professional application development in the terminal environment.\n\nThe page serves as a hub directing developers to specific tutorials addressing common challenges when creating full-featured terminal applications."}]},"uuid":"0b0ee038-fc6d-44ac-b496-c458a9f2c8ef","timestamp":"2026-02-15T11:24:34.590Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b84db5b1-8746-4cc8-9898-bf3957e7459f"}
{"parentUuid":"14e8d4b3-0be8-4183-ae78-a2ebcecdad66","isSidechain":true,"userType":"external","cwd":"/Users/jeevanpillay/Code/@jeevanpillaystudios/dual","sessionId":"b54b5261-ab92-4c9e-9482-0621f9c1b755","version":"2.1.42","gitBranch":"main","agentId":"a62f6a8","slug":"fizzy-whistling-willow","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Ah3cY8kqQquxevAuv4X75a","type":"tool_result","content":"# Crossterm Terminal State Management Pattern\n\nBased on the documentation provided, here's what I can extract about terminal state handling:\n\n## Supported Commands for State Management\n\nThe library offers dedicated commands through its **Command API** for managing terminal state:\n\n**Alternate Screen Management:**\n- `EnterAlternateScreen` - Switches to alternate screen buffer\n- `LeaveAlternateScreen` - Returns to primary screen buffer\n\n**Line Wrapping Control:**\n- `DisableLineWrap` - Prevents automatic line wrapping\n- `EnableLineWrap` - Re-enables line wrapping\n\n**Screen Clearing:**\n- `Clear` command with various `ClearType` options\n\n## Execution Patterns\n\nThe documentation describes two execution approaches:\n\n1. **Direct Execution** - Commands execute immediately via `execute()` trait/macro with automatic flushing\n2. **Lazy Execution** - Commands queue via `queue()` trait/macro, executing only when `flush()` is called\n\n## Important Limitation\n\nThe provided documentation **does not explicitly detail raw mode entry/exit procedures**. While the library clearly supports cursor, color, and screen manipulation, the specific raw mode implementation details are not covered in this page excerpt. You would need to consult the `terminal` module documentation or source code for complete raw mode handling patterns."}]},"uuid":"366de012-272c-40d6-a89d-0ebcd7285bb5","timestamp":"2026-02-15T11:24:37.080Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"14e8d4b3-0be8-4183-ae78-a2ebcecdad66"}

{"parentUuid":"b0758553-e479-45a1-b0e3-0746cf915eff","isSidechain":true,"userType":"external","cwd":"/Users/jeevanpillay/Code/@jeevanpillaystudios/dual","sessionId":"b54b5261-ab92-4c9e-9482-0621f9c1b755","version":"2.1.42","gitBranch":"main","agentId":"a62f6a8","slug":"fizzy-whistling-willow","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01YLXtAMwmZKGuhwUWcYkjDu","type":"tool_result","content":"# Ratatui: A Rust Terminal UI Framework\n\nBased on the GitHub repository content, here's what you should know about Ratatui:\n\n## Overview\n\nRatatui is a Rust crate designed for \"cooking up terminal user interfaces (TUIs).\" It provides developers with tools to create text-based interfaces for command-line applications, dashboards, and interactive console programs.\n\n## Getting Started\n\nThe project offers quick-start templates accessible via `cargo-generate`. A minimal example shows the complete structure needed:\n\n> \"use color_eyre::Result; use crossterm::event::{self, Event}; use ratatui::{DefaultTerminal, Frame};\"\n\nThe framework simplifies initialization with built-in `ratatui::init()` and `ratatui::restore()` functions.\n\n## Documentation & Resources\n\n- **Official Docs**: Available on docs.rs with complete API reference\n- **Website**: ratatui.rs includes concept explanations and tutorials\n- **Examples**: Widget examples and full application demonstrations in the repository\n- **Community**: Discord server, Matrix bridge, and dedicated forum at forum.ratatui.rs\n\n## Project Status\n\n- **Stars**: 18.4k on GitHub\n- **License**: MIT\n- **Contributors**: 267+ developers\n- **Active Development**: 124 releases, with v0.30.0 as the latest (Dec 2025)\n\n## Origins & Community\n\nRatatui was forked from the discontinued tui-rs crate in 2023 to continue active development. The project maintains an active community with multiple communication channels and welcomes contributions, including AI-generated code with guidelines."}]},"uuid":"dca1cc7b-e6e9-4089-b7b2-2f96e525ed96","timestamp":"2026-02-15T11:25:03.139Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"b0758553-e479-45a1-b0e3-0746cf915eff"}

  "content": "# v3.0.0 — Multiplexer Trait + TUI Implementation Plan\n\n## Overview\n\nExtract the tmux module behind a `MultiplexerBackend` trait, then build a ratatui-based TUI as the primary interface. `dual` (no args) launches the TUI. Selecting a workspace suspends the TUI, attaches the tmux session, and resumes the TUI when the user detaches.\n\n## Current State Analysis\n\n**Tmux integration** is 8 public functions in `src/tmux.rs` called directly from `src/main.rs` at 9 call sites. No abstraction layer exists.\n\n**CLI-only interface** — all output via `tracing::info!()` with Unicode status symbols. No TUI dependencies.\n\n### Key Discoveries:\n- `src/tmux.rs` — 8 functions: `is_available`, `create_session`, `attach`, `detach`, `destroy`, `is_alive`, `list_sessions`, `send_keys`, `session_name`\n- `src/main.rs` — 9 tmux call sites across `cmd_launch()`, `cmd_destroy()`, `print_workspace_status()`\n- `src/cli.rs:11` — `command: Option<Command>` already handles `dual` (no args) via `cmd_default()`\n- SPEC.md:269-294 — Backend trait contract with `create_session`, `attach`, `detach`, `destroy`, `is_alive`, `list_sessions`\n- ratatui v0.30.0 — uses `ratatui::init()` / `ratatui::restore()` for terminal state management\n- The TUI → tmux → TUI transition pattern: `restore()` → `tmux attach` (blocks) → tmux detach returns control → `init()` → resume TUI\n\n### The Critical Design Decision: TUI ↔ Tmux Transition\n\n**Model B: Dual manages terminal directly** (chosen)\n\n```\nTerminal\n├── State A: Dual TUI (alternate screen, raw mode)\n│   └── User selects workspace → ratatui::restore() → tmux attach\n└── State B: tmux session (tmux controls terminal)\n    └── User detaches (Ctrl+b d) → tmux returns → ratatui::init() → back to State A\n```\n\nWhy Model B:\n- No nested tmux (Model A's problem)\n- No terminal emulator reimplementation (Model C's problem)\n- Uses ratatui's built-in `init()`/`restore()` for clean state transitions\n- `tmux attach-session` is a blocking call — when user detaches, it returns, and we resume\n\n## Desired End State\n\nAfter v3.0.0:\n1. `dual` → launches TUI with tree view of repos/branches\n2. Arrow keys/j/k to navigate, Enter to select\n3. Selecting a workspace: suspends TUI → launches/attaches tmux session\n4. All `main.rs` code uses `MultiplexerBackend` trait instead of `tmux::*` directly\n\n## What We're NOT Doing\n\n- **Not implementing ZellijBackend** — trait extraction makes it possible, but only TmuxBackend ships in v3.0.0\n- **Not implementing BasicBackend** — deferred to v3.1\n- **Not adding session layout config** — pane/window configuration deferred to v3.1\n- **Not building a full plugin system** — hooks are v3.2+\n- **Not adding fuzzy search in TUI** — simple navigation first, fuzzy filter in v3.1\n- **Not adding meta-key workspace switching** — requires tmux hooks or keybinding config, deferred\n\n---\n\n## Phase 1: Multiplexer Trait Extraction\n\n### Overview\nExtract `src/tmux.rs` functions behind a `MultiplexerBackend` trait. Replace all direct `tmux::*` calls in `main.rs` with trait method calls. The `TmuxBackend` is the only implementation. Zero behavior change — pure refactor.\n\n### Changes Required:\n\n#### 1. Create `src/backend.rs` — Trait Definition\n**File**: `src/backend.rs` (new)\n\n```rust\nuse std::path::Path;\n\n/// Abstraction over terminal multiplexers (tmux, zellij, etc.)\npub trait MultiplexerBackend {\n    /// Check if the multiplexer is installed and available.\n    fn is_available(&self) -> bool;\n\n    /// Create a new detached session with the given name and working directory.\n    /// Optionally run an initial command after creation.\n    fn create_session(\n        &self,\n        session_name: &str,\n        cwd: &Path,\n        init_cmd: Option<&str>,\n    ) -> Result<(), BackendError>;\n\n    /// Attach the current terminal to an existing session.\n    /// If already inside the multiplexer, use switch-client instead.\n    fn attach(&self, session_name: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError>;\n\n    /// Detach the current terminal from a session.\n    fn detach(&self, session_name: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError>;\n\n    /// Destroy a session and all its windows/panes.\n    fn destroy(&self, session_name: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError>;\n\n    /// Check if a session exists and has running processes.\n    fn is_alive(&self, session_name: &str) session_name: &str, keys: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError>;\n\n    /// Generate the session name for a given repo/branch.\n    fn session_name(&self, repo: &str, branch: &str) -> String;\n\n    /// Check if we're currently inside this multiplexer.\n    fn is_inside(&self) -> bool;\n}\n\n#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]\npub enum BackendError {\n    #[error(\"{multiplexer} is not Add Dependencies\n**File**: `Cargo.toml`\n\n```toml\n[dependencies]\nratatui = \"0.30\"\ncrossterm = \"0.28\"\n```\n\nNo tree widget crate needed initially — we'll render the tree manually with ratatui's `List` widget. Create `src/tui/mod.rs` — TUI Module\n**File**: `src/tui/mod.rs` (new)\n\n```rust\nmod app;\nmod ui;\nmod event;\n\npub use app::run;\n```\n\n#### 3. Create `src/tui/app.rs` — App State and Main Loop\n**File**: `src/tui/app.rs` (new)\n\n```rust\nuse crate::backend::MultiplexerBackend;\nuse crate::state::WorkspaceState;\n\npub struct App {\n    /// All workspace entries grouped by repo\n    repos: Vec<RepoGroup>,\n    /// Currently selected index in the flattened list\n    selected: usize,\n    /// Whether the app should exit\n    should_quit: bool,\n    /// Action to take after TUI exits (launch a workspace)\n    pending_action: Option<PendingAction>,\n}\n\npub struct RepoGroup {\n    pub name: String,\n    pub workspaces: Vec<WorkspaceItem>,\n    pub expanded: bool,\n}\n\npub struct WorkspaceItem {\n    pub branch: String,\n    pub workspace_id: String,\n    pub status: WorkspaceStatus,\n}\n\npub enum WorkspaceStatus {\n    Attached,    // tmux session attached\n    Running,     // tmux session exists, not attached\n    Stopped,     // container stopped\n    Lazy,        // not cloned yet\n}\n\npub enum PendingAction {\n    Launch(String), // workspace_id\n}\n\n/// Main TUI entry point.\n/// Returns Ok(Some(workspace_id)) if user selected a workspace to launch.\n/// Returns Ok(None) if user quit.\npub fn run(\n    state: &WorkspaceState,\n    backend: &dyn MultiplexerBackend,\n) -> Result<Option<String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let mut terminal = ratatui::init();\n    let mut app = App::new(state, backend);\n\n    loop {\n        terminal.draw(|frame| ui::render(frame, &app))?;\n\n        if let Some(action) = event::handle_events(&mut app)? {\n            match action {\n                event::Action::Quit => break,\n                event::Action::Select(workspace_id) => {\n                    // Restore terminal before launching tmux\n                    ratatui::restore();\n                    return Ok(Some(workspace_id));\n                }\n            }\n        }\n\n        if app.should_quit {\n            break;\n        }\n    }\n\n    ratatui::restore();\n    Ok(None)\n}\n```\n\n#### 4. Create `src/tui/ui.rs` — Rendering\n**File**: `src/tui/ui.rs` (new)\n\n```rust\nuse ratatui::Frame;\nuse ratatui::layout::{Constraint, Layout};\nuse ratatui::style::{Color, Modifier, Style};\nuse ratatui::text::{Line, Span};\nuse ratatui::widgets::{Block, Borders, List, ListItem, ListState};\n\nuse super::app::{App, WorkspaceStatus};\n\npub fn render(frame: &mut Frame, app: &App) {\n    let area = frame.area();\n\n    // Title block\n    let block = Block::default()\n        .title(\" dual \")\n        .borders(Borders::ALL);\n\n    // Build list items from repo groups\n    let items: Vec<ListItem> = app.flatten_items()\n        .into_iter()\n        .map(|item| {\n            let style = match item.status {\n                Some(WorkspaceStatus::Attached) => Style::default().fg(Color::Green),\n                Some(WorkspaceStatus::Running) => Style::default().fg(Color::Blue),\n                Some(WorkspaceStatus::Stopped) => Style::default().fg(Color::Yellow),\n                Some(WorkspaceStatus::Lazy) => Style::default().fg(Color::DarkGray),\n                None => Style::default().add_modifier(Modifier::BOLD), // repo header\n            };\n            ListItem::new(Line::from(item.display)).style(style)\n        })\n        .collect();\n\n    let list = List::new(items)\n        .block(block)\n        .highlight_style(Style::default().add_modifier(Modifier::REVERSED));\n\n    let mut state = ListState::default();\n    state.select(Some(app.selected));\n\n    frame.render_stateful_widget(list, area, &mut state);\n}\n```\n\n#### 5. Create `src/tui/event.rs` — Event Handling\n**File**: `src/tui/event.rs` (new)\n\n```rust\nuse crossterm::event::{self, Event, KeyCode, KeyEvent};\nuse super::app::App;\n\npub enum Action {\n    Quit,\n    Select(String), // workspace_id\n}\n\npub fn handle_events(app: &mut App) -> Result<Option<Action>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    if event::poll(std::time::Duration::from_millis(100))? {\n        if let Event::Key(key) = event::read()? {\n            return Ok(handle_key(app, key));\n        }\n    }\n    Ok(None)\n}\n\nfn handle_key(app: &mut App, key: KeyEvent) -> Option<Action> {\n    match key.code {\n        KeyCode::Char('q') | KeyCode::Esc => Some(Action::Quit),\n        KeyCode::Up | KeyCode::Char('k') => {\n            app.move_up();\n            None\n        }\n        KeyCode::Down | KeyCode::Char('j') => {\n            app.move_down();\n            None\n        }\n        KeyCode::Enter => {\n            if let Some(workspace_id) = app.selected_workspace_id() {\n                Some(Action::Select(workspace_id))\n            } else {\n                // Selected a repo header — toggle expand/collapse\n                app.toggle_expand();\n                None\n            }\n        }\n        _ => None,\n    }\n}\n```\n\n#### 6. Update `src/main.rs` — Wire TUI as Default\n**File**: `src/main.rs`\n\nChange `cmd_default()` to launch TUI:\n\n```rust\nfn cmd_default(backend: &dyn MultiplexerBackend) -> i32 {\n    let state = match state::load() {\n        Ok(s) => s,\n        Err(e) => {\n            error!(\"Failed to load state: {e}\");\n            return 1;\n        }\n    };\n\n    if state.all_workspaces().is_empty() {\n        info!(\"No workspaces configured. Run `dual add` in a repo to get started.\");\n        return 0;\n    }\n\n    match tui::run(&state, backend) {\n        Ok(Some(workspace_id)) => {\n            // User selected a workspace — launch it\n            cmd_launch_by_id(&workspace_id, backend)\n        }\n        Ok(None) => 0, // User quit\n        Err(e) => {\n            error!(\"TUI error: {e}\");\n            1\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\n#### 7. Update `src/lib.rs` — Export TUI Module\n```rust\npub mod tui;\n```\n\n### Success Criteria:\n\n#### Automated Verification:\n- [ ] `cargo build` — compiles with new dependencies\n- [ ] `cargo test` — all existing tests pass\n- [ ] `cargo clippy` — no warnings\n- [ ] TUI module unit tests pass (app state, navigation, event handling)\n\n#### Manual Verification:\n- [ ] `dual` shows a TUI with repos and branches listed\n- [ ] Arrow keys / j/k navigate the list\n- [ ] Enter on a repo header toggles expand/collapse\n- [ ] Enter on a branch shows workspace status indicators\n- [ ] `q` exits cleanly (terminal restored properly)\n- [ ] `dual list` still works as non-interactive output\n\n**Implementation Note**: After completing this phase and all verification passes, pause here for manual confirmation before proceeding to Phase 3.\n\n---\n\n## Phase 3: TUI ↔ Tmux Transition\n\n### Overview\nWire the TUI selection to the full launch pipeline. When user selects a workspace: suspend TUI → launch workspace (clone/container/session if needed) → attach tmux session → when user detaches, resume TUI.\n\n### Changes Required:\n\n#### 1. Implement the Suspend → Launch → Resume Loop\n**File**: `src/main.rs`\n\nThe key insight: `tmux attach-session` is a **blocking call**. It takes over the terminal and only returns when the user detaches (`Ctrl+b d`). This makes the suspend/resume pattern straightforward:\n\n```rust\nfn cmd_default(backend: &dyn MultiplexerBackend) -> i32 {\n    let mut state = match state::load() { ... };\n\n    loop {\n        // Reload state each iteration (workspaces may have changed)\n        state = match state::load() {\n            Ok(s) => s,\n            Err(e) => { error!(\"{e}\"); return 1; }\n        };\n\n        match tui::run(&state, backend) {\n            Ok(Some(workspace_id)) => {\n                // TUI already called ratatui::restore() before returning\n                // Terminal is now in normal mode — tmux can take over\n\n                // Run the full launch pipeline\n                let exit_code = cmd_launch_by_id(&workspace_id, backend);\n\n                if exit_code != 0 {\n                    // Launch failed — show error, re-enter TUI\n                    eprintln!(\"Launch failed (exit code {exit_code}). Press Enter to continue...\");\n                    let _ = std::io::stdin().read_line(&mut String::new());\n                }\n\n                // When tmux detach returns (or launch failed), loop back to TUI\n                // ratatui::init() is called inside tui::run() at the top\n                continue;\n            }\n            Ok(None) => return 0, // User quit\n            Err(e) => {\n                error!(\"TUI error: {e}\");\n                return 1;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\n#### 2. Update `tui::run()` — Handle Re-entry\n**File**: `src/tui/app.rs`\n\nThe TUI needs to handle being called multiple times in a loop. Each call:\n1. `ratatui::init()` — enter alternate screen\n2. Build fresh app state from workspace state\n3. Run event loop\n4. On selection: `ratatui::restore()` — leave alternate screen\n5. Return selected workspace\n\n```rust\npub fn run(\n    state: &WorkspaceState,\n    backend: &dyn MultiplexerBackend,\n) -> Result<Option<String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let mut terminal = ratatui::init();\n\n    // Build fresh state each time (may have changed since last TUI session)\n    let mut app = App::new(state, backend);\n\n    let result = loop {\n        terminal.draw(|frame| ui::render(frame, &app))?;\n\n        if let Some(action) = event::handle_events(&mut app)? {\n            match action {\n                event::Action::Quit => break Ok(None),\n                event::Action::Select(workspace_id) => {\n                    break Ok(Some(workspace_id));\n                }\n            }\n        }\n    };\n\n    ratatui::restore();\n    result\n}\n```\n\n#### 3. Ensure Proper Terminal Restoration on Panic\n**File**: `src/main.rs`\n\nAdd a panic hook to restore terminal state:\n\n```rust\nfn main() {\n    // Install panic hook BEFORE entering TUI\n    let original_hook = std::panic::take_hook();\n    std::panic::set_hook(Box::new(move |panic_info| {\n        // Best-effort terminal restoration\n        let _ = crossterm::execute!(\n            std::io::stderr(),\n            crossterm::terminal::LeaveAlternateScreen\n        );\n        let _ = crossterm::terminal::disable_raw_mode();\n        original_hook(panic_info);\n    }));\n\n    // ... rest of main\n}\n```\n\n#### 4. Handle \"Already Inside Tmux\" Case in TUI\n**File**: `src/tui/app.rs`\n\nIf the user launches `dual` from inside a tmux session, the TUI still works. When they select a workspace, `backend.attach()` uses `switch-client` (from Phase 1). But after switching, the TUI process is still running in the original tmux session's pane.\n\nTwo options:\n- **Option A**: Exit TUI after switch-client (user returns to TUI by running `dual` again)\n- **Option B**: Keep TUI running, user switches back to it via tmux\n\nWe choose **Option A** for simplicity:\n\n```rust\nevent::Action::Select(workspace_id) => {\n    if backend.is_inside() {\n        // Inside tmux — switch-client won't block, so exit TUI\n        ratatui::restore();\n        cmd_launch_by_id(&workspace_id, backend);\n        return Ok(None); // Don't loop back — tmux switch is instant\n    } else {\n        // Outside tmux — attach blocks until detach\n        break Ok(Some(workspace_id));\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Success Criteria:\n\n#### Automated Verification:\n- No performance concern.\n- **State loading**: `state::load()` reads `~/.dual/workspaces.toml` (small file). Fine on each TUI loop iteration.\n- **Status checks**: `backend.is_alive()` shells out to `tmux has-session` for each workspace. With 15 workspaces, this is 15 subprocess calls (~150ms total). Acceptable for TUI entry, but v3.1 should cache and refresh on a timer.\n- **Container status**: `container::status()` shells out to `docker inspect`. ## Summary

**3 phases to v3.0.0:**

**Phase 1: Multiplexer Trait Extraction** (pure refactor, zero behavior change)
- New `src/backend.rs` — `MultiplexerBackend` trait with 9 methods
- New `src/tmux_backend.rs` — `TmuxBackend` implementing the trait
- Replace all 9 `tmux::*` call sites in `main.rs` with `backend.*`
- Delete `src/tmux.rs`

**Phase 2: TUI Scaffold** (new feature, additive)
- Add `ratatui` + `crossterm` dependencies
- New `src/tui/` module — app state, tree view rendering, keyboard navigation
- `dual` (no args) → TUI with repos/branches tree
- `dual list` → non-interactive fallback (unchanged)

**Phase 3: TUI ↔ Tmux Transition** (the critical integration)
- User selects workspace in TUI → `ratatui::restore()` (leave alternate screen) → full launch pipeline → `tmux attach` (blocks)
- User detaches from tmux → `tmux attach` returns → loop back → `ratatui::init()` → TUI resumes
- Panic hook for terminal state restoration
- Inside-tmux detection: use `switch-client` path instead of suspend/resume loop

**Key design decision**: Model B (Dual manages terminal directly). The TUI and tmux take turns controlling the terminal — never both at once, no nesting.

Please review — any changes to the phasing, scope, or technical approach?

    23→**Model B: Dual manages terminal directly** (chosen)

    25→```
    26→Terminal

    63→### Overview

    69→**File**: `src/backend.rs` (new)
    70→
    71→```rust
    72→use std::path::Path;
    73→
    74→/// Abstraction over terminal multiplexers (tmux, zellij, etc.)
    75→pub trait MultiplexerBackend {
    76→    /// Check if the multiplexer is installed and available.
    77→    fn is_available(&self) -> bool;
    78→
    79→    /// Create a new detached session with the given name and working directory.
    80→    /// Optionally run an initial command after creation.
    81→    fn create_session(
    82→        &self,
    83→        session_name: &str,
    84→        cwd: &Path,
    85→        init_cmd: Option<&str>,
    86→    ) -> Result<(), BackendError>;
    87→
    88→    /// Attach the current terminal to an existing session.
    89→    /// If already inside the multiplexer, use switch-client instead.
    90→    fn attach(&self, session_name: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError>;
    91→
    92→    /// Detach the current terminal from a session.
    93→    fn detach(&self, session_name: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError>;
    94→
    95→    /// Destroy a session and all its windows/panes.
    96→    fn destroy(&self, session_name: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError>;
    97→
    98→    /// Check if a session exists and has running processes.
    99→    fn is_alive(&self, session_name: &str) -> bool;
   100→
   101→    /// List all sessions managed by Dual (filtered by prefix).
   102→    fn list_sessions(&self) -> Vec<String>;
   103→
   104→    /// Send keystrokes to a session's active pane.
   105→    fn send_keys(&self, session_name: &str, keys: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError>;
   106→
   107→    /// Generate the session name for a given repo/branch.
   108→    fn session_name(&self, repo: &str, branch: &str) -> String;
   109→
   110→    /// Check if we're currently inside this multiplexer.
   111→    fn is_inside(&self) -> bool;
   112→}
   113→
   114→#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
   115→pub enum BackendError {
   116→    #[error("{multiplexer} is not installed")]
   117→    NotFound { multiplexer: String },
   118→
   119→    #[error("{operation} failed for session '{session}': {stderr}")]
   120→    Failed {
   121→        operation: String,
   122→        session: String,
   123→        stderr: String,
   124→    },
   125→}
   126→```
   127→

   134→pub struct TmuxBackend;
   135→
   136→impl TmuxBackend {

   138→        Self
   139→    }
   140→}
   141→
   142→impl MultiplexerBackend for TmuxBackend {
   143→    fn is_available(&self) -> bool {
   144→        // existing tmux::is_available() logic
   145→    }
   146→
   147→    fn attach(&self, session_name: &str) -> Result<(), BackendError> {
   148→        // Use switch-client if is_inside(), otherwise attach-session
   149→        if self.is_inside() {
   150→            // tmux switch-client -t {session_name}
   151→        } else {
   152→            // tmux attach-session -t {session_name}
   153→        }
   154→    }
   155→
   156→    fn is_inside(&self) -> bool {
   157→        std::env::var("TMUX").is_ok()
   158→    }
   159→
   160→    // ... rest of trait methods wrapping existing tmux.rs functions
   161→}
   162→```

   254→#### 3. Create `src/tui/app.rs` — App State and Main Loop
   255→**File**: `src/tui/app.rs` (new)
   256→
   257→```rust
   258→use crate::backend::MultiplexerBackend;
   259→use crate::state::WorkspaceState;
   260→
   261→pub struct App {
   262→    /// All workspace entries grouped by repo
   263→    repos: Vec<RepoGroup>,
   264→    /// Currently selected index in the flattened list
   265→    selected: usize,
   266→    /// Whether the app should exit
   267→    should_quit: bool,
   268→    /// Action to take after TUI exits (launch a workspace)
   269→    pending_action: Option<PendingAction>,
   270→}
   271→
   272→pub struct RepoGroup {
   273→    pub name: String,
   274→    pub workspaces: Vec<WorkspaceItem>,
   275→    pub expanded: bool,
   276→}
   277→
   278→pub struct WorkspaceItem {
   279→    pub branch: String,
   280→    pub workspace_id: String,
   281→    pub status: WorkspaceStatus,
   282→}
   283→
   284→pub enum WorkspaceStatus {
   285→    Attached,    // tmux session attached
   286→    Running,     // tmux session exists, not attached
   287→    Stopped,     // container stopped
   288→    Lazy,        // not cloned yet
   289→}
   290→
   291→pub enum PendingAction {
   292→    Launch(String), // workspace_id
   293→}
   294→
   295→/// Main TUI entry point.
   296→/// Returns Ok(Some(workspace_id)) if user selected a workspace to launch.
   297→/// Returns Ok(None) if user quit.
   298→pub fn run(
   299→    state: &WorkspaceState,
   300→    backend: &dyn MultiplexerBackend,
   301→) -> Result<Option<String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   302→    let mut terminal = ratatui::init();
   303→    let mut app = App::new(state, backend);
   304→
   305→    loop {
   306→        terminal.draw(|frame| ui::render(frame, &app))?;
   307→
   308→        if let Some(action) = event::handle_events(&mut app)? {
   309→            match action {
   310→                event::Action::Quit => break,
   311→                event::Action::Select(workspace_id) => {
   312→                    // Restore terminal before launching tmux
   313→                    ratatui::restore();
   314→                    return Ok(Some(workspace_id));
   315→                }
   316→            }
   317→        }
   318→
   319→        if app.should_quit {
   320→            break;
   321→        }
   322→    }
   323→
   324→    ratatui::restore();
   325→    Ok(None)
   326→}
   327→```
   328→
   329→#### 4. Create `src/tui/ui.rs` — Rendering
   330→**File**: `src/tui/ui.rs` (new)
   331→
   332→```rust
   333→use ratatui::Frame;
   334→use ratatui::layout::{Constraint, Layout};
   335→use ratatui::style::{Color, Modifier, Style};
   336→use ratatui::text::{Line, Span};
   337→use ratatui::widgets::{Block, Borders, List, ListItem, ListState};
   338→
   339→use super::app::{App, WorkspaceStatus};
   340→
   341→pub fn render(frame: &mut Frame, app: &App) {
   342→    let area = frame.area();
   343→
   344→    // Title block
   345→    let block = Block::default()
   346→        .title(" dual ")
   347→        .borders(Borders::ALL);
   348→
   349→    // Build list items from repo groups
   350→    let items: Vec<ListItem> = app.flatten_items()
   351→        .into_iter()
   352→        .map(|item| {
   353→            let style = match item.status {
   354→                Some(WorkspaceStatus::Attached) => Style::default().fg(Color::Green),
   355→                Some(WorkspaceStatus::Running) => Style::default().fg(Color::Blue),
   356→                Some(WorkspaceStatus::Stopped) => Style::default().fg(Color::Yellow),
   357→                Some(WorkspaceStatus::Lazy) => Style::default().fg(Color::DarkGray),
   358→                None => Style::default().add_modifier(Modifier::BOLD), // repo header
   359→            };
   360→            ListItem::new(Line::from(item.display)).style(style)
   361→        })
   362→        .collect();
   363→
   364→    let list = List::new(items)
   365→        .block(block)
   366→        .highlight_style(Style::default().add_modifier(Modifier::REVERSED));
   367→
   368→    let mut state = ListState::default();
   369→    state.select(Some(app.selected));
   370→
   371→    frame.render_stateful_widget(list, area, &mut state);
   372→}
   373→```
   374→
   375→#### 5. Create `src/tui/event.rs` — Event Handling
   376→**File**: `src/tui/event.rs` (new)
   377→
   378→```rust
   379→use crossterm::event::{self, Event, KeyCode, KeyEvent};
   380→use super::app::App;
   381→
   382→pub enum Action {
   383→    Quit,
   384→    Select(String), // workspace_id
   385→}
   386→
   387→pub fn handle_events(app: &mut App) -> Result<Option<Action>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   388→    if event::poll(std::time::Duration::from_millis(100))? {
   389→        if let Event::Key(key) = event::read()? {
   390→            return Ok(handle_key(app, key));
   391→        }
   392→    }
   393→    Ok(None)
   394→}
   395→
   396→fn handle_key(app: &mut App, key: KeyEvent) -> Option<Action> {
   397→    match key.code {
   398→        KeyCode::Char('q') | KeyCode::Esc => Some(Action::Quit),
   399→        KeyCode::Up | KeyCode::Char('k') => {
   400→            app.move_up();
   401→            None
   402→        }
   403→        KeyCode::Down | KeyCode::Char('j') => {
   404→            app.move_down();
   405→            None
   406→        }
   407→        KeyCode::Enter => {
   408→            if let Some(workspace_id) = app.selected_workspace_id() {
   409→                Some(Action::Select(workspace_id))
   410→            } else {
   411→                // Selected a repo header — toggle expand/collapse
   412→                app.toggle_expand();
   413→                None
   414→            }
   415→        }
   416→        _ => None,
   417→    }
   418→}
   419→```
   420→

   422→**File**: `src/main.rs`

   424→Change `cmd_default()` to launch TUI:
   425→
   426→```rust
   427→fn cmd_default(backend: &dyn MultiplexerBackend) -> i32 {
   428→    let state = match state::load() {
   429→        Ok(s) => s,
   430→        Err(e) => {
   431→            error!("Failed to load state: {e}");
   432→            return 1;
   433→        }
   434→    };
   435→
   436→    if state.all_workspaces().is_empty() {
   437→        info!("No workspaces configured. Run `dual add` in a repo to get started.");
   438→        return 0;
   439→    }
   440→
   441→    match tui::run(&state, backend) {
   442→        Ok(Some(workspace_id)) => {
   443→            // User selected a workspace — launch it
   444→            cmd_launch_by_id(&workspace_id, backend)
   445→        }
   446→        Ok(None) => 0, // User quit
   447→        Err(e) => {
   448→            error!("TUI error: {e}");
   449→            1
   450→        }
   451→    }
   452→}
   453→```

   479→
   480→## Phase 3: TUI ↔ Tmux Transition
   481→
   482→### Overview
   483→Wire the TUI selection to the full launch pipeline. When user selects a workspace: suspend TUI → launch workspace (clone/container/session if needed) → attach tmux session → when user detaches, resume TUI.
   484→
   485→### Changes Required:
   486→
   487→#### 1. Implement the Suspend → Launch → Resume Loop
   488→**File**: `src/main.rs`
   489→
   490→The key insight: `tmux attach-session` is a **blocking call**. It takes over the terminal and only returns when the user detaches (`Ctrl+b d`). This makes the suspend/resume pattern straightforward:
   491→
   492→```rust
   493→fn cmd_default(backend: &dyn MultiplexerBackend) -> i32 {
   494→    let mut state = match state::load() { ... };
   495→
   496→    loop {
   497→        // Reload state each iteration (workspaces may have changed)
   498→        state = match state::load() {
   499→            Ok(s) => s,
   500→            Err(e) => { error!("{e}"); return 1; }
   501→        };
   502→
   503→        match tui::run(&state, backend) {
   504→            Ok(Some(workspace_id)) => {
   505→                // TUI already called ratatui::restore() before returning
   506→                // Terminal is now in normal mode — tmux can take over
   507→
   508→                // Run the full launch pipeline
   509→                let exit_code = cmd_launch_by_id(&workspace_id, backend);
   510→
   511→                if exit_code != 0 {
   512→                    // Launch failed — show error, re-enter TUI
   513→                    eprintln!("Launch failed (exit code {exit_code}). Press Enter to continue...");
   514→                    let _ = std::io::stdin().read_line(&mut String::new());
   515→                }
   516→
   517→                // When tmux detach returns (or launch failed), loop back to TUI
   518→                // ratatui::init() is called inside tui::run() at the top
   519→                continue;
   520→            }
   521→            Ok(None) => return 0, // User quit
   522→            Err(e) => {
   523→                error!("TUI error: {e}");
   524→                return 1;
   525→            }
   526→        }
   527→    }
   528→}
   529→```
   530→
   531→#### 2. Update `tui::run()` — Handle Re-entry
   532→**File**: `src/tui/app.rs`
   533→
   534→The TUI needs to handle being called multiple times in a loop. Each call:
   535→1. `ratatui::init()` — enter alternate screen
   536→2. Build fresh app state from workspace state
   537→3. Run event loop
   538→4. On selection: `ratatui::restore()` — leave alternate screen
   539→5. Return selected workspace
   540→
   541→```rust
   542→pub fn run(
   543→    state: &WorkspaceState,
   544→    backend: &dyn MultiplexerBackend,
   545→) -> Result<Option<String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   546→    let mut terminal = ratatui::init();
   547→
   548→    // Build fresh state each time (may have changed since last TUI session)
   549→    let mut app = App::new(state, backend);
   550→
   551→    let result = loop {
   552→        terminal.draw(|frame| ui::render(frame, &app))?;
   553→
   554→        if let Some(action) = event::handle_events(&mut app)? {
   555→            match action {
   556→                event::Action::Quit => break Ok(None),
   557→                event::Action::Select(workspace_id) => {
   558→                    break Ok(Some(workspace_id));
   559→                }
   560→            }
   561→        }
   562→    };
   563→
   564→    ratatui::restore();
   565→    result
   566→}
   567→```
   568→
   569→#### 3. Ensure Proper Terminal Restoration on Panic
   570→**File**: `src/main.rs`
   571→
   572→Add a panic hook to restore terminal state:
   573→
   574→```rust
   575→fn main() {
   576→    // Install panic hook BEFORE entering TUI
   577→    let original_hook = std::panic::take_hook();
   578→    std::panic::set_hook(Box::new(move |panic_info| {
   579→        // Best-effort terminal restoration
   580→        let _ = crossterm::execute!(
   581→            std::io::stderr(),
   582→            crossterm::terminal::LeaveAlternateScreen
   583→        );
   584→        let _ = crossterm::terminal::disable_raw_mode();
   585→        original_hook(panic_info);
   586→    }));
   587→
   588→    // ... rest of main
   589→}
   590→```
   591→
   592→#### 4. Handle "Already Inside Tmux" Case in TUI
   593→**File**: `src/tui/app.rs`
   594→
   595→If the user launches `dual` from inside a tmux session, the TUI still works. When they select a workspace, `backend.attach()` uses `switch-client` (from Phase 1). But after switching, the TUI process is still running in the original tmux session's pane.
   596→
   597→Two options:
   598→- **Option A**: Exit TUI after switch-client (user returns to TUI by running `dual` again)
   599→- **Option B**: Keep TUI running, user switches back to it via tmux
   600→
   601→We choose **Option A** for simplicity:
   602→
   603→```rust
   604→event::Action::Select(workspace_id) => {
   605→    if backend.is_inside() {
   606→        // Inside tmux — switch-client won't block, so exit TUI
   607→        ratatui::restore();
   608→        cmd_launch_by_id(&workspace_id, backend);
   609→        return Ok(None); // Don't loop back — tmux switch is instant
   610→    } else {
   611→        // Outside tmux — attach blocks until detach
   612→        break Ok(Some(workspace_id));
   613→    }
   614→}
   615→```
   616→
   617→### Success Criteria:
   618→
   619→#### Automated Verification:
   620→- [ ] `cargo test` — all tests pass
   621→- [ ] `cargo clippy` — no warnings
   622→- [ ] Panic hook test — TUI restores terminal on panic
   623→
   624→#### Manual Verification:
   625→- [ ] `dual` → TUI → select workspace → tmux session appears
   626→- [ ] Detach from tmux (Ctrl+b d) → TUI reappears automatically
   627→- [ ] Select another workspace → switches to it
   628→- [ ] `q` in TUI → exits cleanly
   629→- [ ] Run `dual` from inside tmux → TUI shows → select workspace → tmux switches client
   630→- [ ] If launch fails (e.g., container not available), error message shown, TUI resumes
   631→- [ ] Ctrl+C during TUI → terminal restored properly
   632→- [ ] Panic during TUI → terminal restored properly
   633→
   634→**Implementation Note**: After completing this phase and all verification passes, this constitutes the v3.0.0 release.
   635→
   636→---

   668→
   669→## Testing Strategy
   670→
   671→### Unit Tests:
   672→- `backend.rs`: Trait definition compiles, error types work
   673→- `tmux_backend.rs`: All existing tmux tests migrated, `is_inside()` tested with env var mock
   674→- `tui/app.rs`: App state construction from WorkspaceState, navigation (up/down/expand/collapse), selected_workspace_id()
   675→- `tui/event.rs`: Key event → action mapping
   676→- `tui/ui.rs`: Render doesn't panic for empty state, single repo, multiple repos
   677→
   678→### Integration Tests:
   679→- Full TUI → launch → detach → resume cycle (requires tmux, marked `#[ignore]`)
   680→- TUI exit restores terminal (check raw mode disabled, alternate screen left)
   681→
   682→### Manual Testing Steps:
   683→1. `dual` with no workspaces → helpful message, no TUI crash
   684→2. `dual` with workspaces → TUI with tree view
   685→3. Navigate with j/k and arrow keys
   686→4. Enter on repo header → expand/collapse
   687→5. Enter on branch → full launch pipeline → tmux session
   688→6. Ctrl+b d → TUI returns
   689→7. Select another branch → switches
   690→8. q → exits, terminal clean
   691→9. Run from inside tmux → switch-client behavior
   692→10. Kill TUI with Ctrl+C → terminal clean
   693→
   694→## Performance Considerations
   695→
   696→- **TUI startup**: ratatui::init() is ~1ms. No performance concern.
   697→- **State loading**: `state::load()` reads `~/.dual/workspaces.toml` (small file). Fine on each TUI loop iteration.
   698→- **Status checks**: `backend.is_alive()` shells out to `tmux has-session` for each workspace. With 15 workspaces, this is 15 subprocess calls (~150ms total). Acceptable for TUI entry, but v3.1 should cache and refresh on a timer.
   699→- **Container status**: `container::status()` shells out to `docker inspect`. Same concern — cache in v3.1.
   700→
   701→## Migration Notes
   702→
   703→### v2.2.0 → v3.0.0
   704→- **`dual` (no args) behavior change**: Was `cmd_default()` showing list output. Now launches TUI.
   705→- **`dual list` unchanged**: Non-interactive fallback, same output as before.
   706→- **New dependencies**: `ratatui` (~1.5MB), `crossterm` (~500KB). Adds to binary size.
   707→- **`src/tmux.rs` removed**: Replaced by `src/backend.rs` + `src/tmux_backend.rs`. All logic preserved.
   708→- **No config changes**: `.dual.toml` format unchanged. `workspaces.toml` unchanged.
   709→

     1→use std::path::Path;
     2→use std::process::Command;
     3→
     4→/// Session name prefix for dual-managed sessions.
     5→const SESSION_PREFIX: &str = "dual-";
     6→
     7→/// Check if tmux is available on the system.
     8→pub fn is_available() -> bool {
     9→    Command::new("tmux")
    10→        .arg("-V")
    11→        .output()
    12→        .is_ok_and(|o| o.status.success())
    13→}
    14→
    15→/// Create a new detached tmux session for a workspace.
    16→///
    17→/// - Session name follows the container naming convention: dual-{repo}-{branch}
    18→/// - Working directory is set to the workspace clone dir
    19→/// - Optionally sources shell RC for command interception
    20→pub fn create_session(
    21→    session_name: &str,
    22→    cwd: &Path,
    23→    shell_rc: Option<&str>,
    24→) -> Result<(), TmuxError> {
    25→    // Create detached session with CWD
    26→    let output = Command::new("tmux")
    27→        .args(build_new_session_args(session_name, cwd))
    28→        .output()
    29→        .map_err(|e| TmuxError::NotFound(e.to_string()))?;
    30→
    31→    if !output.status.success() {
    32→        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).to_string();
    33→        return Err(TmuxError::Failed {
    34→            operation: "new-session".to_string(),
    35→            session: session_name.to_string(),
    36→            stderr,
    37→        });
    38→    }
    39→
    40→    // Source shell RC if provided
    41→    if let Some(rc) = shell_rc {
    42→        send_keys(session_name, rc)?;
    43→    }
    44→
    45→    Ok(())
    46→}
    47→
    48→/// Attach to an existing tmux session.
    49→///
    50→/// If already inside tmux (`$TMUX` is set), uses `switch-client` to avoid
    51→/// nested sessions. Otherwise uses `attach-session`.
    52→pub fn attach(session_name: &str) -> Result<(), TmuxError> {
    53→    let (cmd, op) = if is_inside_tmux() {
    54→        (["switch-client", "-t", session_name], "switch-client")
    55→    } else {
    56→        (["attach-session", "-t", session_name], "attach-session")
    57→    };
    58→
    59→    let status = Command::new("tmux")
    60→        .args(cmd)
    61→        .status()
    62→        .map_err(|e| TmuxError::NotFound(e.to_string()))?;
    63→
    64→    if !status.success() {
    65→        return Err(TmuxError::Failed {
    66→            operation: op.to_string(),
    67→            session: session_name.to_string(),
    68→            stderr: format!("exit code: {}", status.code().unwrap_or(-1)),
    69→        });
    70→    }
    71→
    72→    Ok(())
    73→}
    74→
    75→/// Check if we're already inside a tmux session.
    76→pub fn is_inside_tmux() -> bool {
    77→    std::env::var("TMUX").is_ok_and(|v| !v.is_empty())
    78→}
    79→
    80→/// Detach the current client from a session.
    81→pub fn detach(session_name: &str) -> Result<(), TmuxError> {
    82→    tmux_simple(&["detach-client", "-s", session_name])
    83→}
    84→
    85→/// Destroy a tmux session (kill all panes and processes).
    86→pub fn destroy(session_name: &str) -> Result<(), TmuxError> {
    87→    tmux_simple(&["kill-session", "-t", session_name])
    88→}
    89→
    90→/// Check if a session exists and is alive.
    91→pub fn is_alive(session_name: &str) -> bool {
    92→    Command::new("tmux")
    93→        .args(["has-session", "-t", session_name])
    94→        .output()
    95→        .is_ok_and(|o| o.status.success())
    96→}
    97→
    98→/// List all dual-managed tmux sessions.
    99→/// Returns session names that start with the dual- prefix.
   100→pub fn list_sessions() -> Vec<String> {
   101→    let output = Command::new("tmux")
   102→        .args(["list-sessions", "-F", "#{session_name}"])
   103→        .output();
   104→
   105→    match output {
   106→        Ok(out) if out.status.success() => {
   107→            let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stdout);
   108→            stdout
   109→                .lines()
   110→                .filter(|line| line.starts_with(SESSION_PREFIX))
   111→                .map(|s| s.to_string())
   112→                .collect()
   113→        }
   114→        _ => Vec::new(),
   115→    }
   116→}
   117→
   118→/// Send keys (a command string) to a tmux session pane.
   119→pub fn send_keys(session_name: &str, keys: &str) -> Result<(), TmuxError> {
   120→    tmux_simple(&["send-keys", "-t", session_name, keys, "Enter"])
   121→}
   122→
   123→/// Build the arguments for tmux new-session (for testing).
   124→pub fn build_new_session_args(session_name: &str, cwd: &Path) -> Vec<String> {
   125→    vec![
   126→        "new-session".to_string(),
   127→        "-d".to_string(),
   128→        "-s".to_string(),
   129→        session_name.to_string(),
   130→        "-c".to_string(),
   131→        cwd.to_string_lossy().to_string(),
   132→    ]
   133→}
   134→
   135→/// Generate the session name for a workspace.
   136→pub fn session_name(repo: &str, branch: &str) -> String {
   137→    use crate::config::encode_branch;
   138→    format!("{SESSION_PREFIX}{repo}-{}", encode_branch(branch))
   139→}
   140→
   141→fn tmux_simple(args: &[&str]) -> Result<(), TmuxError> {
   142→    let output = Command::new("tmux")
   143→        .args(args)
   144→        .output()
   145→        .map_err(|e| TmuxError::NotFound(e.to_string()))?;
   146→
   147→    if !output.status.success() {
   148→        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).to_string();
   149→        return Err(TmuxError::Failed {
   150→            operation: args.first().unwrap_or(&"unknown").to_string(),
   151→            session: args
   152→                .iter()
   153→                .position(|&a| a == "-t" || a == "-s")
   154→                .and_then(|i| args.get(i + 1))
   155→                .unwrap_or(&"unknown")
   156→                .to_string(),
   157→            stderr,
   158→        });
   159→    }
   160→
   161→    Ok(())
   162→}
   163→
   164→#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
   165→pub enum TmuxError {
   166→    #[error("tmux not found: {0}")]
   167→    NotFound(String),
   168→
   169→    #[error("tmux {operation} failed for {session}: {stderr}")]
   170→    Failed {
   171→        operation: String,
   172→        session: String,
   173→        stderr: String,
   174→    },
   175→}
